Good. Now I have all the data I need. Here is the meta-report.

---

# META-REPORT: Desloppify Fresh User Experience

## What the Scan Output Told Me (Section by Section)

The scan output was 91 lines. Here is what each section communicated:

**1. Zone summary (line 1):** `Zones: config: 1, production: 351, script: 85, test: 204` -- This tells me the tool classified 641 files into four zones. I understood "production" and "test" immediately. "script" was less clear -- does it mean standalone scripts, or something else? "config" at 1 file seems oddly low.

**2. Detector progress (lines 2-30):** The tool ran 15 detectors sequentially, printing a one-liner per detector with counts. This was informative and felt like a proper progress log. I could see it was doing real work: unused imports, structural analysis, responsibility cohesion, coupling analysis, test coverage, code smells, security checks, duplication. The counts gave me a rough scale of the codebase. This section was well-designed -- compact, honest, no fluff.

**3. Total line:** `Total: 727 findings` -- Clear and useful as an anchor number.

**4. Delta summary:** `+3 new / -4 resolved / 6 reopened` -- Very useful. Tells me this is an incremental scan, not a first run. The "Skipped auto-resolve for: review (returned 0 -- likely transient)" warning was opaque. What does "returned 0" mean in practice? Why would that be transient?

**5. Score summary:** `overall 90.6/100 objective 95.0/100 (-0.1) strict 90.6/100 verified 95.0/100 (-0.1)` -- Four different scores on one line. This was confusing. What is the difference between "overall", "objective", "strict", and "verified"? As a first-time user, I had no mental model for why there are four scores or which one I should care about. The (-0.1) delta was helpful but only applied to two of the four.

**6. Noise budget note:** `604 findings hidden (showing 10/detector)` -- Practical and appreciated. Tells me how to dig deeper.

**7. Scorecard dimensions (20 dimensions, lines ~15-50):** This was the meatiest section. A visual bar chart with 20 dimensions ranging from 48.0% (Abstraction fit) to 99.9% (Duplication). The bars were easy to scan visually. The `[stale -- re-review]` tags told me 12 of 20 subjective dimensions need refreshing. The split between mechanical (File health, Code quality, Duplication, Test health, Security) and subjective dimensions was not explicitly labeled until the status command. I had to infer it.

**8. Score recipe:** This was the most useful analytical section. It told me the overall score is 40% mechanical + 60% subjective, and then listed the "biggest weighted drags" with exact point penalties. This is excellent -- it directly answers "what should I focus on to improve the score?" The answer was clear: Contracts (-2.05 pts), Abstraction fit (-2.03 pts), File health (-1.24 pts).

**9. Movement delta:** `Test health 94.4% -> 94.2% (-0.2%)` -- Good to see what changed. Only one dimension moved, which makes sense for an incremental scan.

**10. Chronic reopeners note:** `178 chronic reopeners` -- Useful warning about findings that keep coming back.

**11. Final coaching paragraph:** `59 security findings -- review before other cleanup. Score plateaued at 90.6 for 5 scans. Abstraction fit (48.0%) is where the breakthrough is.` -- This was the best line in the entire output. It told me three things in one sentence: (a) there is a security backlog, (b) the score has been stuck, and (c) where the leverage is. Immediately actionable.

**12. Next commands:** `Run desloppify next` and `Run desloppify status` -- Clear calls to action.

## What I Didn't Understand

1. **Four score types (overall/objective/strict/verified):** The scan never explains what these mean or which one matters. I later pieced together from the status command that "Health = open penalized" and "Strict = open + wontfix penalized" but "overall" vs "objective" vs "verified" remained unclear. Are "objective" and "verified" the same thing? Why show both?

2. **Tier system:** The tiers (T1-T4) appeared in the output but were not defined until I ran `plan`. Even then, the tier descriptions in parentheses were brief. T1 is "auto-fixable" but the Tier 1 items in the plan output were all "Design review (requires judgment)" findings from the holistic review. That seems like a misclassification -- those are not auto-fixable.

3. **"Script" zone:** 85 files classified as "script." I never found out what makes a file a "script" vs "production." The zone command was available but the classification criteria were opaque.

4. **Subjective vs mechanical dimensions:** The scan output lists all 20 dimensions in one block. The status command adds a separator line ("Subjective Measures") but the scan does not. As a new user I did not immediately understand that "Abstraction fit" is an LLM-assessed dimension while "File health" is computed mechanically, or what that distinction implies for actionability.

5. **"phantom_read" dict_keys findings:** Many of the top-priority T2 items were `Dict key "X" read at line Y but never written to \`payload\``. These are flagging `.get()` calls on function parameters -- the dict is passed in from outside, so the detector cannot see the write site. It feels like a high false-positive rate for this detector on function parameters.

6. **The `--attest` flag on resolve:** The resolve command requires an `--attest` string that must describe what you did. The example text reads like a forced confession: "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing." This is hostile to the user. It communicates distrust. I understand the anti-gaming motivation but the tone is off-putting.

7. **"Noise budget: 10/detector"**: What is a noise budget? Why 10? Can I change it?

## What I Did Next (and Why)

| Command | Why I Ran It | Was It Useful? |
|---------|-------------|---------------|
| `next` | Scan told me to. | Yes -- showed me the single highest-priority item with a concrete suggestion. |
| `status` | Scan told me to. | Moderately -- it was a longer version of the scan's scorecard. The "Structural Debt by Area" table was new and useful. The dimension table was a repeat. |
| `plan` | Mentioned in the project memory. | The output was 157KB (thousands of lines). Overwhelming. The first 40 lines were useful (dimension table + tier summary). The remaining 3000+ lines were an exhaustive finding-by-finding list. I stopped reading at line 120. |
| `show review --status open` | Scan told me to ("10 review findings"). | Very useful -- showed me the 10 holistic review findings with concrete descriptions and file references. |
| `show security --status open` | Scan warned about "59 security findings." | Moderately useful. The visible 10 were all low-severity bandit warnings (B404, B101, B311). The 49 hidden ones are probably more of the same. Not urgent despite the warning tone. |
| `show smells --status open` | Status suggested it. | Useful -- monster functions and swallowed errors are real problems. |
| `show structural --status open` | Status suggested it. | Useful -- large files needing decomposition. Concrete and actionable. |
| `show --chronic` | Scan mentioned "178 chronic reopeners." | Less useful than expected -- mostly "unreviewed" subjective_review findings that re-open because no review has ever been done. These are not truly "chronic" bugs; they are just the review backlog masquerading as reopened findings. |
| `show orphaned --status open` | Status AGENT PLAN suggested it. | Useful but tiny -- just one orphaned file (`go/move.py`). Quick win. |
| `show flat_dirs --status open` | Curiosity. | Useful -- identified 7 overloaded directories. Actionable for reorganization. |
| `show subjective` | Scan footnote mentioned it. | Returned "No open findings matching: subjective." This was confusing. I expected it to show the subjective dimension details. The detector name is apparently `subjective_review`, not `subjective`. |

## Did the Scan Give Me a Clear Next Step?

**Yes, eventually.** The final coaching paragraph was excellent: "Abstraction fit (48.0%) is where the breakthrough is." The score recipe section backed this up with the weighted-drag analysis. Running `next` confirmed it by showing me the top-priority review finding.

However, there is a tension in the output. It simultaneously tells me:
- "59 security findings -- review before other cleanup" (suggesting security is urgent)
- "Abstraction fit is where the breakthrough is" (suggesting subjective review is the priority)
- "Run `desloppify next` for the highest-priority item" (which showed a review finding, not a security finding)

These three signals partially conflict. Security is framed as urgent but the `next` command deprioritizes it (security findings are T3/T4). The coaching says to review security "before other cleanup" but the scoring says Abstraction fit is the biggest drag. A new user would be confused about which to do first.

**The actual clear next step** that I would take: fix the one unused import (T1, mechanical, trivially auto-fixable), then address the 10 holistic review findings since they are T1/T2 and affect the lowest-scoring dimensions.

## How Many Lines of Output Did I Have to Read Before Finding Actionable Info?

- **Scan (91 lines total):** The first actionable line was the score at line ~35. The truly actionable coaching was at line ~82. So roughly **80 lines** before the "here is what to do" message. The detector progress (lines 1-30) is informational but not actionable.

- **Next (24 lines total):** Actionable in the first 10 lines. This is the most efficient command.

- **Status (86 lines total):** The dimension table (lines ~10-35) is the core. The AGENT PLAN section near the end (~line 70) is highly actionable. The middle section repeats the score recipe from scan.

- **Plan (3000+ lines):** The first 37 lines are the only ones worth reading. Everything after that is a finding-by-finding dump that belongs in a file, not terminal output.

## What Was the Signal-to-Noise Ratio?

**Scan: 7/10.** Most of the output is signal. The detector progress lines are useful. The dimension table is signal. The score recipe is high signal. The noise comes from: four unexplained score variants on one line, and the "[stale -- re-review]" tags that appear on 12/20 dimensions making the whole scorecard feel unreliable.

**Next: 9/10.** Almost pure signal. One finding, one suggestion, one resolve command. The resolve command's `--attest` boilerplate is the only noise.

**Status: 5/10.** Half is a repeat of scan output. The Structural Debt by Area table and the AGENT PLAN are new and high value. The dimension table is duplicated from scan.

**Plan: 2/10.** The header table is excellent. The tier-by-tier exhaustive dump is appropriate for a markdown file export but terrible for terminal reading. This command should probably default to summary mode and require `--full` or `--detail` for the exhaustive listing.

**Show commands: 8/10.** These are well-focused. You ask for a detector, you get findings for that detector. The 10/detector noise budget is sensible. The AGENT PLAN footer that repeats on every show command is noise -- I saw it 8 times across my session.

## Suggestions for Improvement

### High Priority

1. **Explain the four scores somewhere.** Add a one-line legend to the scan output: "overall = weighted blend, objective = mechanical only, strict = penalizes wontfix, verified = attested fixes only" or whatever they mean. Currently they are inscrutable.

2. **Fix the plan command's default output.** It should print the summary table and tier counts, then say "Full plan written to .desloppify/plan.md" (or similar). Dumping 3000+ lines to a terminal is not useful.

3. **Resolve the conflicting priority signals.** The scan says "security first" but `next` says "review finding first." Pick one voice. Either the coaching paragraph should match the `next` ranking, or explain why they differ ("security findings are lower tier but should be audited for real vulnerabilities").

4. **Reduce the AGENT PLAN footer repetition.** It appears identically at the bottom of every `show` command. After the third time, it is pure noise. Show it once or make it a separate command.

5. **The `--attest` language is hostile.** Rephrase from "I am not gaming the score" to something like "Change description: [what you did]." Anti-gaming can be enforced mechanically (e.g., requiring a git diff hash) rather than through accusatory language.

### Medium Priority

6. **Label mechanical vs subjective dimensions in scan output.** The status command has a separator; the scan should too.

7. **Make `show subjective` work.** If users type `show subjective` and get nothing, that is a discoverability bug. Either alias it to `show subjective_review` or make the dimension names and detector names match.

8. **The "phantom_read" dict_keys detector has a high false-positive rate on function parameters.** When a function receives a dict parameter and reads keys from it, the detector flags those reads as "never written." This is technically correct (within the function scope) but practically misleading. Consider suppressing phantom_read findings on function parameters, or at least lowering their confidence.

9. **Tier 1 should actually be auto-fixable.** Currently, holistic review findings (which require human judgment) are classified as T1. This undermines the tier system's meaning. T1 items should be things `desloppify fix` can handle automatically.

10. **Chronic reopeners are inflated by the unreviewed backlog.** 178 "chronic reopeners" sounds alarming, but most are just files that have never had a subjective review. These should not be in the chronic list -- they are not reopened bugs, they are outstanding review tasks. Separate "never reviewed" from "was fixed but broke again."

### Low Priority

11. **Explain "noise budget" somewhere.** It is a good concept but the term is jargon. A parenthetical would help: "Noise budget: 10/detector (use `show <detector>` to see all)."

12. **The scorecard.png badge generation is mentioned at the end of every scan.** Most users will not care about this. Consider suppressing the message unless `--badge-path` is explicitly passed.

13. **`show --chronic` output truncation.** The command showed 15 findings and then cut off. It told me there were 178 but I only saw 15 and had to guess how to see the rest.

### Overall Impression

Desloppify is a sophisticated tool that does real, useful analysis. The scan-then-explore workflow (scan -> status/next/show) is well-designed. The `next` command is the star of the UX -- it gives you exactly one thing to do with no ambiguity. The `show` commands are focused and useful.

The main UX problems are: (a) information overload on `scan` and especially `plan`, (b) undefined terminology (four score types, tiers, zones, noise budget), (c) conflicting priority signals between the coaching text and the ranking engine, and (d) the repetitive AGENT PLAN footer. The tool would benefit from a "first run" mode that explains its concepts, and a stricter default-to-summary approach for commands that can produce thousands of lines.

---

## META-REPORT

### What I Did (Step by Step, What Commands I Ran and Why)

1. **`python -m desloppify --lang python scan --path desloppify`** -- Ran an initial scan to populate state. This was the obvious first step and the MEMORY.md instructions confirmed it.

2. **`python -m desloppify --lang python next --path desloppify`** -- Tried to run `next` with `--path`. This failed because `next` does not accept `--path`. Had to retry without it. First friction point.

3. **`python -m desloppify --lang python next`** -- Got the top recommendation: a review finding about TypedDict contract softening.

4. **`python -m desloppify --lang python plan`** -- Ran plan to see the full prioritized breakdown. Output was very large (157KB) but gave useful context on the dimension table and tier breakdown.

5. **`python -m desloppify --lang python show review --status open`** -- Showed all 10 open review findings with related files listed.

6. **Read the two files cited by the top finding** (`import_helpers.py` and `scan_workflow.py`) to understand the actual code.

7. **`python -m desloppify --lang python next --tier 1 --count 6`** -- Showed all 6 T1 items to understand what else is in the queue.

8. **`python -m desloppify --lang python status`** -- Got the full dashboard view with dimension scores, structural debt breakdown, and weighted drags.

9. **`python -m desloppify --lang python next --explain`** -- Used the `--explain` flag to try to understand why this item was ranked #1. The explain output was minimal: just `ranked by tier=1, confidence=high, count=0, id=...`.

10. **Inspected the StateModel TypedDict** to validate the finding's claim about `.get()` misuse.

11. **Checked the subjective assessment data** for abstraction_fitness to understand the score composition.

### What Confused Me

1. **`next` does not accept `--path` but `scan` does.** There is no obvious reason why. This caused a wasted step. The error message just says "unrecognized arguments" without suggesting the correct usage.

2. **The top recommendation is a "design review" finding, not a code fix.** The finding says "Type: Design review (requires judgment)" and has `File: .` (codebase-wide). As a developer, I expected "the single most important thing to work on" to be something actionable -- a specific file to change, a specific pattern to fix. Instead it is a subjective design critique that requires broad architectural judgment.

3. **The finding's evidence is partially inaccurate.** The finding claims the scan_workflow.py helpers "auto-replace non-dict state with empty dicts, erasing malformed persisted state." But `_state_subjective_assessments` and `_state_findings` both RAISE `ScanStateContractError` on non-dict values -- they do NOT silently swallow. Only `_state_lang_capabilities` creates an empty dict on None, and that field is not even in the `StateModel` TypedDict (it is truly optional). The `import_helpers.py` side is more accurate -- `_normalize_import_payload_shape` does use `.get()` with defaults -- but it also collects and returns errors, so it is not truly silent.

4. **The relationship between "review findings" and "subjective dimension scores" is unclear.** Abstraction fit is at 48%, and there are 4 open review findings tagged to it. But the tool never says: "fixing these 4 findings would raise the score from 48% to X%." I have no idea what the score impact of resolving this one finding would be.

5. **The "AGENT PLAN" in the show/status output recommends different things than `next`.** The AGENT PLAN says "Agent focus: show orphaned -- 1 orphaned finding," while `next` says the top priority is the TypedDict contract softening finding. These are contradictory. Which one is the actual recommendation?

6. **The scan output's coaching says "59 security findings -- review before other cleanup"** but `next` does not surface any security findings as priority. The scan says "Abstraction fit (48.0%) is where the breakthrough is" -- which aligns with the `next` output, but contradicts the security warning in the same breath.

7. **`show --verbose` does not exist.** I tried it to get more detail on the finding. There is no way to get deeper evidence or reasoning from the tool beyond what is in the finding's `detail.evidence` array.

### What Was Missing

1. **Score impact projection.** I wanted to know: "If I fix this one finding, what does the overall score become?" The tool never tells me. The dimensions are subjective and scored by an LLM review, so resolving a finding does not mechanically change the score. This makes the whole priority ranking feel disconnected from outcomes.

2. **Concrete code locations.** The finding says `File: .` (codebase-wide) and lists 2 "related files." But it does not show specific line numbers or code snippets. I had to manually read the files to find the patterns. For the unused import (finding #6), the tool DOES show inline code with line numbers. The experience gap between mechanical and review findings is large.

3. **"How to fix" guide.** The suggestion says "Introduce strict boundary constructors... validate required keys explicitly, fail with structured errors." But it does not say WHERE to make these changes, how many call sites exist, or what a good pattern looks like. For an AI agent being asked to fix this, there is not enough to proceed without substantial exploration.

4. **Dependency information.** Would fixing this finding unblock other improvements? Are any other findings related to or caused by this same pattern? No information is provided on finding-to-finding relationships.

5. **Effort estimation.** No indication of how much work this represents. The plan output shows tier breakdown but does not estimate effort per finding.

6. **Historical context.** The finding was `first_seen` today (during my scan). It is brand new. Was this always an issue? Did the score used to be higher? No trend data is surfaced by `next`.

### Do I Trust the Recommendation?

**Partially, but with significant reservations.**

**What I trust:** The tool correctly identifies that the `abstraction_fitness` dimension (48.0%) is the biggest weighted drag on the overall score. The score recipe in the status output confirms this. It makes sense to focus there.

**What I do not trust:**

1. **The specific finding is partially wrong.** The evidence claims scan_workflow helpers silently swallow bad state, but two of the three helpers actually raise errors. The finding overstates the problem.

2. **The finding is a subjective review opinion, not an objective measurement.** It was generated by an LLM review process. The dimension score (48%) was also LLM-assigned. There is a circular quality: an LLM said the abstraction fitness is poor, then an LLM generated findings about what is poor, then the tool ranks those findings as highest priority because the score is low. The human-verifiable ground truth is thin.

3. **Resolving this finding may not change the score.** Since the score is subjective and assigned by a future LLM review run, fixing this particular `.get()` pattern might not move the abstraction_fitness score at all if the reviewer decides other patterns are more important. The link between "fix finding" and "score improves" is not mechanical.

4. **The unused import (finding #6) is objectively higher-value per unit effort.** It is a 10-second fix with guaranteed score impact on the Code Quality dimension. The tool ranks it below 5 design review findings that each require significant architectural judgment.

### What I Would Actually Do With This Information

If I were a developer on this codebase:

1. **First**, I would fix the unused import in `scan_coverage.py` (T1 #6). It is trivial, objectively correct, and moves the score.

2. **Second**, I would look at the other 5 T1 review findings (flat_dirs denominator mismatch, single_use contract overstatement, viz exception swallowing, warning/error severity mixing) and triage them. The `flat_dirs_denominator_mismatch` and `single_use_contract_overstates_behavior` findings sound like concrete, verifiable bugs that I could investigate.

3. **Third**, for the TypedDict contract softening finding specifically, I would probably resolve it as `wontfix` with a note explaining that the scan_workflow.py helpers already DO raise on invalid shapes, and the import_helpers.py validation is defensively coded but does propagate errors. The finding is exaggerated.

4. I would NOT follow the top recommendation blindly. The "highest priority" item is actually the one I trust least.

### How Long It Took Me to Go From "Run the Tool" to "I Know What to Do"

**Approximately 15-20 minutes of wall clock time across 18 steps.** The breakdown:

- ~2 minutes: Initial scan (waiting for it to complete)
- ~1 minute: Running `next` (including the failed `--path` attempt)
- ~3 minutes: Running `plan`, `status`, `show` to get broader context
- ~8 minutes: Reading the actual code files to verify the finding
- ~3 minutes: Investigating the score mechanics and dimension details

If I had stopped at step 3 (`next` output) and just trusted the tool, I would have had a recommendation in under 3 minutes but would have been working on something partially wrong. The verification step was essential and the tool did not help with it.

### Suggestions for Improvement

1. **Show score impact projection.** Even if approximate: "Resolving this finding contributes to the abstraction_fitness dimension (48.0%). Improving this dimension to 70% would raise overall score from 90.6 to ~91.8." Give me a reason to care about this specific finding.

2. **Make `next` consistent with `--path` handling across commands.** Either all commands accept `--path` or none do. The inconsistency is a papercut.

3. **Rank trivially-fixable items above design-review items** (at least optionally). A flag like `--actionable-first` or `--quick-wins` would help. The current ranking puts hard-to-verify subjective findings above easy-to-verify mechanical ones, which optimizes for theoretical impact but not for developer flow.

4. **Show inline code in review findings.** The mechanical findings show code snippets with line numbers. The review findings only show file names. Since review findings require the most judgment, they should provide the MOST evidence, not the least.

5. **Validate evidence accuracy.** The finding's evidence about scan_workflow.py is materially inaccurate. If the review LLM is generating evidence claims, those claims should be spot-checked against the actual code before being surfaced as findings.

6. **Resolve the contradiction between AGENT PLAN and `next`.** The AGENT PLAN says "focus on orphaned findings" while `next` says "focus on TypedDict contract softening." These should agree, or the AGENT PLAN should not appear alongside `next` output.

7. **Add `--explain` depth.** The current `--explain` output is just one line: `ranked by tier=1, confidence=high, count=0`. Useful explain output would include: why this tier, what dimension it impacts, how the dimension score was computed, what the estimated effort is, and what happens to the score if it is resolved.

8. **Surface staleness warnings on `next` findings.** The abstraction_fitness dimension is marked `[stale -- re-review]` in the scan output. That means the 48% score might not even be current. Yet `next` does not mention this -- it presents the finding as urgent without noting that the underlying score assessment is stale.

> AGENT

---

# META-REPORT: Exploring Desloppify as a Fresh User

## What I Did (step by step, what commands I ran and why)

1. **`scan --path desloppify`** -- The obvious first step. Ran a full scan to get a baseline. Got: overall 90.6/100, 727 findings across 641 files. This gave me the scorecard dimensions and the "biggest weighted drags" summary.

2. **`status`** -- Wanted the full dashboard. Got the same score data in a different format, plus a structural debt breakdown by area, tier distribution (T1:6, T2:89, T3:405, T4:248), and a clear pointer to the "AGENT PLAN" section that kept recommending I look at orphaned files, smells, and structural findings.

3. **`next`** -- Asked for the single highest-priority item. Got a Tier 1 design review finding about TypedDict contract softening. This was a subjective/review finding, not something I could auto-fix.

4. **`plan`** -- Generated the full prioritized breakdown. This was very long (157KB). It confirmed the dimension table and gave me Tier 1-4 grouped task lists.

5. **`next --count 20`** -- Got 20 items in priority order. 5 Tier 1 items (all review findings), then Tier 2 items (dict_keys phantom reads, schema drift, unused vars).

6. **`show review --status open`** -- 10 open review findings, all holistic/codebase-wide. These are the high-value subjective findings.

7. **`show subjective`** -- Tried to get subjective dimension details. Got "No open findings matching: subjective." This was confusing -- I expected it to show me the dimension scores and what drives them.

8. **`show smells --status open --top 20`** -- 146 total smells, saw monster functions, silent excepts, empty excepts, swallowed errors, noop functions.

9. **`show structural --status open --top 20`** -- 131 structural findings, all "needs decomposition: large (XXX LOC)" type findings.

10. **`show security --status open --top 20`** -- 59 security findings, mostly bandit warnings (B404 subprocess, B101 assert, B311 random, etc.).

11. **`show dict_keys --status open --top 20`** -- 34 dict_keys findings. Phantom reads and schema drift.

12. **`show test_coverage`, `show facade`, `show orphaned`, `show uncalled_functions`, `show cycles`, `show flat_dirs`, `show private_imports`, `show responsibility_cohesion`, `show global_mutable_config`** -- Explored each detector category to understand the full landscape.

13. **`fix --help`** -- Discovered there are NO auto-fixers for Python. TypeScript has 6 fixers, Python has zero.

14. **`review --help`** and **`review --prepare`** -- Explored the subjective review system. It generates investigation batches that need to be run through external runners (Codex or Claude). I could not actually run a review without `codex` CLI or a Claude external session.

15. **`config show`** -- Checked configuration. Target strict score is 98. Badge path is set. Everything else is default.

16. **Inspected `query.json`** -- Dug into the data model to understand score composition. Score = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective. Mechanical pool average 95.0%, subjective pool average 87.7%.

17. **Inspected narrative/strategy data** -- Found the phase is "stagnation" (score plateaued for 5 scans). All action impacts are 0.0. The tool says "Abstraction fit (48.0%) is where the breakthrough is."


## What Confused Me

1. **All action impacts are 0.0.** Every single recommended action in the strategy has `impact: 0.0`. This is deeply confusing. If I fix 131 structural findings, how many points do I gain? The tool cannot tell me. The "Biggest weighted drags" section at the top gives numbers like "-2.05 pts" for Contracts and "-2.03 pts" for Abstraction fit, but individual actions don't have estimated point values. There's a fundamental disconnect: the tool tells me what to fix but cannot estimate what fixing it would do to the score.

2. **Mechanical vs subjective score confusion.** The score is 40% mechanical + 60% subjective, but the subjective dimensions are largely "stale" (12 out of 15). The tool keeps telling me to re-review, but re-reviewing requires an external runner (Codex CLI or Claude external session). So 60% of my score is locked behind an external process I cannot easily run from this CLI session.

3. **`show subjective` returns nothing.** I expected this to show me the subjective dimension breakdown. Instead it says "No open findings matching: subjective" and just prints the dimension scores in the footer. The data exists, but the command feels like a dead end.

4. **What does "re-review" actually mean for scores?** When a dimension is "stale", does that mean the score could go UP or DOWN if I re-review? If I've improved the code since the last review, re-reviewing might raise scores. But the tool doesn't clarify this.

5. **The Tier system vs actual impact.** Items are prioritized by Tier, but it's not clear what determines the tier. The T1 items are all holistic review findings (design judgment), while actual code-fixable issues (unused imports, dead functions) are T2-T3. This means the "most urgent" items require the most human judgment and cannot be automated.

6. **"Score plateaued at 90.6 for 5 scans" -- what broke the progression?** The tool tells me I'm stagnating but doesn't explain what changed between scans or why the easy gains have been exhausted. There's no history view.

7. **Zone classification of "script" on many production files.** Files in `desloppify/app/commands/` are classified as "script" zone. This might affect how findings are weighted but it's not clear whether this helps or hurts the score.


## What Was Missing

1. **Score projection.** "If I fix these 34 dict_keys findings, the Code quality dimension would go from 96.9% to ~97.8% and overall from 90.6 to ~90.9." I wanted this and it doesn't exist. The `impact: 0.0` on every action is essentially "we don't know."

2. **Score history / trend chart.** I know the score has been 90.6 for 5 scans. But I can't see the history. When did it plateau? What was the trajectory before?

3. **Finding count by detector in a single summary.** I had to run `show` for each detector individually. Something like `status --by-detector` that shows "smells: 146, structural: 131, security: 59, responsibility_cohesion: 57, ..." in one view would save a lot of exploration.

4. **Subjective dimension explanation.** "Abstraction fit: 48.0%" -- why? What evidence was used? The review findings give some hints (4 open issues), but 48% out of a 10-check pool with 4 issues doesn't make mathematical sense (4/10 = 60% failing = 60% health? but it says 48%). The score formula for subjective dimensions is opaque.

5. **Auto-fixer for Python.** The `fix` command has zero Python fixers. For a Python codebase, this is a major gap. Unused imports, dead functions, and some code smells could be auto-fixed.

6. **What "resolving" a finding actually does to the score.** If I `resolve fixed` a finding, does it contribute the same as if it were auto-resolved by the scanner? Does `resolve wontfix` hurt the strict score? The distinction between `health` and `strict` scores is explained briefly but not quantitatively.

7. **A "what-if" mode.** "If I wontfix all 59 security findings, what happens to the score?" vs "If I fix them all?"


## What Stopped Me

1. **Cannot run subjective reviews.** The biggest score lever (60% of overall) is the subjective review system, which requires either the Codex CLI (`--runner codex`) or starting a Claude external session (`--external-start --external-runner claude`). Neither is something I can trivially do in this context. This means the most impactful action is gated behind external infrastructure.

2. **No Python auto-fixers.** Unlike TypeScript with 6 fixers, Python has zero. I was told "Action: fix" for Code quality, but `fix` has nothing to offer for Python.

3. **Cannot actually modify code (by instruction).** But even if I could, the highest-priority items (T1) are design judgment calls that require careful refactoring, not simple fixes.

4. **The attestation anti-gaming system.** To resolve any finding, you need an `--attest` flag with specific phrasing. This is good for integrity but creates friction for legitimate workflows. I cannot batch-resolve findings that are genuinely false positives without typing an attestation for each.

5. **The noise budget hides most findings.** `show smells` only shows 10 and hides 136. `show structural` shows 10 and hides 121. I had to piece together the full picture from footer summaries. While I understand the UX rationale, when trying to understand what's dragging the score, I wanted the full list (or at least a `--top 0` for unlimited).


## The Strategy I Came Up With (and how confident I am in it)

**Current score: 90.6/100. Target: 98.0. Gap: 7.4 points.**

**Score formula: 40% mechanical (95.0%) + 60% subjective (87.7%) = 90.6%**

To reach 98.0, I need the weighted average to be 98.0. If mechanical stays at 95.0, subjective needs to be 100.0 (40%*95 + 60%*100 = 98.0). That's unrealistic. So both need to improve.

**Phase 1: Mechanical improvements (target: raise mechanical from 95.0% to ~97%)**
- Fix the 1 unused import (Tier 1, trivial)
- Remove the 6 uncalled private functions (dead code removal)
- Fix the 34 dict_keys findings (phantom reads suggest the dict_keys detector is flagging function parameters read via `.get()` -- many might be false positives, but some are genuine)
- Fix the 2 unused vars
- Address the 10 most impactful structural findings (decompose the largest files)
- Confidence: **Medium.** I'm not sure how much each finding moves the needle since impact is always 0.0.

**Phase 2: Subjective re-reviews (target: raise subjective from 87.7% to ~95%+)**
- Re-run holistic reviews on the 12 stale dimensions, especially:
  - **Abstraction fit (48.0%)** -- The biggest subjective drag. 4 open issues, 10 checks. If the code has improved since the last review, re-reviewing could dramatically raise this.
  - **Contracts (65.0%)** -- 3 open issues. Fix the 3 specific contract coherence findings (flat_dirs denominator, single_use contract, SectionPayload equality), then re-review.
  - **Error consistency (65.3%)** -- 3 open issues. Fix the viz broad exception swallowing and warning/error severity mixing, then re-review.
  - **Stale migration (81.5%)**, **Logic clarity (84.1%)**, **AI generated debt (85.8%)** -- Re-review to potentially improve.
- Confidence: **Low-medium.** Subjective reviews are 60% of the score, but I cannot control what scores the reviewer gives. If the code genuinely has abstraction problems, re-reviewing won't help.

**Phase 3: Fix-then-re-review cycle**
- Fix the 10 open review findings (the concrete code issues identified by past reviews)
- Re-scan to pick up the fixes
- Re-run subjective reviews to capture the improvements
- Confidence: **Medium.** This is the loop the tool seems designed for, but it requires multiple scan/review/fix cycles.

**Overall confidence: Medium.** The strategy is clear in theory but the execution depends heavily on the subjective review system, which I cannot run myself. The mechanical side is more tractable but represents only 40% of the score.


## What the Tool Told Me to Do vs What I Think I Should Do

**Tool says:**
1. "Show orphaned findings" (1 orphaned file) -- primary action
2. Re-review stale subjective dimensions via `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`
3. Fix security findings first ("review before other cleanup")
4. "Abstraction fit (48.0%) is where the breakthrough is"

**What I think I should do:**
1. **Disagree on priority of orphaned file.** 1 orphaned file is trivial to fix but has near-zero score impact. The tool prioritizes it because it's an easy win in the "restructure" lane, but it won't move the needle.
2. **Agree that subjective re-review is the breakthrough.** 60% of the score is subjective. 12 of 15 dimensions are stale. If even a few improve after re-review, that's the biggest possible gain.
3. **Partially disagree on security first.** Security has 59 findings but the dimension is already at 97.6%. Fixing all 59 might raise it to 99%+ but the weighted impact is small. The tool flags it because of the finding count, not the score impact.
4. **Agree that Abstraction fit is key.** At 48.0%, it's the lowest dimension by far. But the 4 open issues there are design-level problems (TypedDict contract softening, facade sprawl, runtime contract triplication, string-routed dispatch). These require substantial refactoring, not quick fixes.
5. **I'd prioritize the 10 open review findings first,** then re-review. Fix what the last review found, demonstrate improvement, then ask the reviewer to look again. The tool somewhat suggests this in the "fix then rescan" workflow, but the primary action guidance doesn't emphasize it.


## Suggestions for Improvement

### High Priority

1. **Score projection / impact estimates.** Every action currently shows `impact: 0.0`. This is the single biggest UX gap. Even rough estimates ("fixing this could move Code quality from 96.9% to ~97.2%") would be transformative for prioritization. Without this, the user is flying blind.

2. **Detector summary view.** Add a `status --by-detector` or `summary` command that shows all detectors with their open finding counts in one table. Right now you have to run `show <detector>` for each of the 50+ detectors individually.

3. **Python auto-fixers.** The `fix` command having zero Python fixers is a significant gap. Unused imports (via ruff --fix), dead functions, and some code smells are trivially auto-fixable.

4. **Clarify subjective score mechanics.** How exactly is "Abstraction fit: 48.0%" computed from 10 checks and 4 issues? What are the 10 "checks"? Are they the 10 dimensions of a holistic review? A brief explanation in `show` output or `plan` would help enormously.

### Medium Priority

5. **Score history.** A `history` or `trend` command showing the score over the last N scans. Right now I know it's been 90.6 for 5 scans, but I can't see the trajectory that got here.

6. **"What if" mode for resolve.** Something like `resolve --simulate wontfix security` that shows "this would change Security from 97.6% to X% and overall from 90.6 to Y%."

7. **Better noise budget UX.** When 136/146 smell findings are hidden, offer a way to see the full list without guessing. Maybe `show smells --all` or `show smells --top 0`. The current `--top 20` still only shows 10 because of the noise budget.

8. **`show subjective` should work.** When I type `show subjective`, I expect to see the 15 subjective dimensions with their scores, staleness, and what contributes to each. Instead I get "no findings matching."

9. **Phase explanation.** The narrative says `phase: stagnation`. This should be surfaced more prominently with an explanation: "Your score has been 90.6 for 5 consecutive scans. To break out, you need to [specific action]."

### Low Priority

10. **Batch resolve with single attestation.** For legitimate batch operations (e.g., "all B404 subprocess import warnings are expected in this CLI tool"), allow a single attestation for a batch rather than requiring per-finding attestation.

11. **Explain the tier system.** What determines T1 vs T2 vs T3 vs T4? The labels ("Auto-fixable", "Quick fixes", "Needs judgment", "Major refactors") are helpful but the actual assignment logic is unclear, especially when T1 contains subjective design review findings that are anything but "auto-fixable."

12. **Scan diff from previous.** The scan shows "+3 new, -4 resolved, 6 reopened" but doesn't show which specific findings changed. A `scan --diff` or `show --new` command would help.

13. **The `review --prepare` output is confusing for newcomers.** It generates investigation batches and tells you to run Codex or Claude, but if you don't have those set up, you're stuck. A clearer error or fallback ("you need Codex CLI installed, run `pip install codex`") would help.

```
1→"""plan command: generate prioritized markdown plan from state."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.rendering import print_agent_plan
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
    10→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import warn_best_effort
    11→from desloppify.engine import planning as planning_mod
    12→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    13→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    14→
    15→
    16→def cmd_plan_output(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    17→    """Generate a prioritized markdown plan from state."""
    18→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    19→
    20→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    21→        return
    22→
    23→    plan_md = planning_mod.generate_plan_md(state)
    24→    next_command = "desloppify next --count 20"
    25→
    26→    output = getattr(args, "output", None)
    27→    if output:
    28→        try:
    29→            safe_write_text(output, plan_md)
    30→            print(colorize(f"Plan written to {output}", "green"))
    31→            print_agent_plan(["Inspect and execute the generated plan."], next_command=next_command)
    32→        except OSError as e:
    33→            warn_best_effort(f"Could not write plan to {output}: {e}")
    34→    else:
    35→        print(plan_md)
    36→        print()
    37→        print_agent_plan(["Start from the top-ranked action in this plan."], next_command=next_command)
    38→
```

```
1→"""Score and diff summary output for scan command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_helpers import _format_delta
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.status_parts.strict_target import (
    10→    format_strict_target_progress,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→
    17→def _consecutive_subjective_integrity_status(state: dict, status: str) -> int:
    18→    """Return consecutive trailing scans with the given subjective-integrity status."""
    19→    history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    20→    if not isinstance(history, list):
    21→        return 0
    22→
    23→    streak = 0
    24→    for entry in reversed(history):
    25→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    26→            break
    27→        integrity = entry.get("subjective_integrity")
    28→        if not isinstance(integrity, dict):
    29→            break
    30→        if integrity.get("status") != status:
    31→            break
    32→        streak += 1
    33→    return streak
    34→
    35→
    36→def show_diff_summary(diff: dict):
    37→    """Print the +new / -resolved / reopened one-liner."""
    38→    diff_parts = []
    39→    if diff["new"]:
    40→        diff_parts.append(colorize(f"+{diff['new']} new", "yellow"))
    41→    if diff["auto_resolved"]:
    42→        diff_parts.append(colorize(f"-{diff['auto_resolved']} resolved", "green"))
    43→    if diff["reopened"]:
    44→        diff_parts.append(colorize(f"↻{diff['reopened']} reopened", "red"))
    45→    if diff_parts:
    46→        print(f"  {' · '.join(diff_parts)}")
    47→    else:
    48→        print(colorize("  No changes since last scan", "dim"))
    49→    if diff.get("suspect_detectors"):
    50→        print(
    51→            colorize(
    52→                "  ⚠ Skipped auto-resolve for: "
    53→                f"{', '.join(diff['suspect_detectors'])} (returned 0 — likely transient)",
    54→                "yellow",
    55→            )
    56→        )
    57→
    58→
    59→def show_score_delta(
    60→    state: dict,
    61→    prev_overall: float | None,
    62→    prev_objective: float | None,
    63→    prev_strict: float | None,
    64→    prev_verified: float | None = None,
    65→    non_comparable_reason: str | None = None,
    66→):
    67→    """Print the canonical score quartet with deltas."""
    68→    stats = state["stats"]
    69→    new = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    70→
    71→    if (
    72→        new.overall is None
    73→        or new.objective is None
    74→        or new.strict is None
    75→        or new.verified is None
    76→    ):
    77→        print(
    78→            colorize(
    79→                "  Scores unavailable — run a full scan with language detectors enabled.",
    80→                "yellow",
    81→            )
    82→        )
    83→        return
    84→
    85→    overall_delta_str, overall_color = _format_delta(new.overall, prev_overall)
    86→    objective_delta_str, objective_color = _format_delta(new.objective, prev_objective)
    87→    strict_delta_str, strict_color = _format_delta(new.strict, prev_strict)
    88→    verified_delta_str, verified_color = _format_delta(new.verified, prev_verified)
    89→    print(
    90→        "  Scores: "
    91→        + colorize(f"overall {new.overall:.1f}/100{overall_delta_str}", overall_color)
    92→        + colorize(
    93→            f"  objective {new.objective:.1f}/100{objective_delta_str}",
    94→            objective_color,
    95→        )
    96→        + colorize(f"  strict {new.strict:.1f}/100{strict_delta_str}", strict_color)
    97→        + colorize(
    98→            f"  verified {new.verified:.1f}/100{verified_delta_str}",
    99→            verified_color,
   100→        )
   101→    )
   102→    if isinstance(non_comparable_reason, str) and non_comparable_reason.strip():
   103→        print(colorize(f"  Δ non-comparable: {non_comparable_reason.strip()}", "yellow"))
   104→    # Surface wontfix debt gap prominently when significant
   105→    wontfix = stats.get("wontfix", 0)
   106→    gap = (new.overall or 0) - (new.strict or 0)
   107→    if gap >= 5 and wontfix >= 10:
   108→        print(
   109→            colorize(
   110→                f"  ⚠ {gap:.1f}-point gap between overall and strict — "
   111→                f"{wontfix} wontfix items represent hidden debt",
   112→                "yellow",
   113→            )
   114→        )
   115→
   116→    integrity = state.get("subjective_integrity", {})
   117→    if isinstance(integrity, dict):
   118→        status = integrity.get("status")
   119→        matched_count = int(integrity.get("matched_count", 0) or 0)
   120→        target = integrity.get("target_score")
   121→        if status == "penalized":
   122→            print(
   123→                colorize(
   124→                    "  ⚠ Subjective integrity: "
   125→                    f"{matched_count} target-matched dimensions were reset to 0.0 "
   126→                    f"({'target ' + str(target) if target is not None else 'target threshold'}).",
   127→                    "red",
   128→                )
   129→            )
   130→            streak = _consecutive_subjective_integrity_status(state, "penalized")
   131→            if streak >= 2:
   132→                print(
   133→                    colorize(
   134→                        "    Repeated penalty across scans. Use a blind, isolated reviewer "
   135→                        "on `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` and re-import before trusting subjective scores.",
   136→                        "yellow",
   137→                    )
   138→                )
   139→        elif status == "warn":
   140→            print(
   141→                colorize(
   142→                    "  ⚠ Subjective integrity: "
   143→                    f"{matched_count} dimension matched the target "
   144→                    f"({'target ' + str(target) if target is not None else 'target threshold'}). Re-review recommended.",
   145→                    "yellow",
   146→                )
   147→            )
   148→            streak = _consecutive_subjective_integrity_status(state, "warn")
   149→            if streak >= 2:
   150→                print(
   151→                    colorize(
   152→                        "    This warning has repeated. Prefer "
   153→                        "`desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` "
   154→                        "or run a blind reviewer pass before import.",
   155→                        "yellow",
   156→                )
   157→            )
   158→
   159→
   160→def show_concern_count(state: dict, lang_name: str | None = None) -> None:
   161→    """Print concern count if any exist."""
   162→    try:
   163→        from desloppify.engine.concerns import generate_concerns
   164→
   165→        concerns = generate_concerns(state, lang_name=lang_name)
   166→        if concerns:
   167→            print(
   168→                colorize(
   169→                    f"  {len(concerns)} potential design concern{'s' if len(concerns) != 1 else ''}"
   170→                    " (run `show concerns` to view)",
   171→                    "cyan",
   172→                )
   173→            )
   174→    except (ImportError, AttributeError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   175→        logger.debug("Concern generation failed (best-effort): %s", exc)
   176→
   177→
   178→def show_strict_target_progress(strict_target: dict | None) -> tuple[float | None, float | None]:
   179→    """Print strict target progress lines and return (target, gap)."""
   180→    lines, target, gap = format_strict_target_progress(strict_target)
   181→    for message, style in lines:
   182→        print(colorize(message, style))
   183→    return target, gap
   184→
   185→
   186→__all__ = ["show_concern_count", "show_diff_summary", "show_score_delta", "show_strict_target_progress"]
   187→
```

```
1→"""LLM-facing reporting helpers for scan command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import os
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify import scoring as scoring_mod
    10→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_reporting_text import build_workflow_guide
    12→from desloppify.engine.planning import scorecard_projection as scorecard_projection_mod
    13→from desloppify.core import registry as registry_mod
    14→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    15→from desloppify.core import skill_docs as skill_docs_mod
    16→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→def _is_agent_environment() -> bool:
    22→    return bool(
    23→        os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE")
    24→        or os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_AGENT")
    25→        or os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI")
    26→        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED")
    27→        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX")
    28→        or os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID")
    29→    )
    30→
    31→
    32→def _load_scores(state: dict) -> state_mod.ScoreSnapshot:
    33→    """Load all four canonical scores from state."""
    34→    return state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    35→
    36→
    37→def _print_score_lines(
    38→    *,
    39→    overall_score: float | None,
    40→    objective_score: float | None,
    41→    strict_score: float | None,
    42→    verified_score: float | None,
    43→) -> None:
    44→    lines: list[str] = []
    45→    if overall_score is not None:
    46→        lines.append(f"Overall score:   {overall_score:.1f}/100")
    47→    if objective_score is not None:
    48→        lines.append(f"Objective score: {objective_score:.1f}/100")
    49→    if strict_score is not None:
    50→        lines.append(f"Strict score:    {strict_score:.1f}/100")
    51→    if verified_score is not None:
    52→        lines.append(f"Verified score:  {verified_score:.1f}/100")
    53→    if lines:
    54→        print("\n".join(lines))
    55→    print()
    56→
    57→
    58→def _split_dimension_scores(
    59→    state: dict,
    60→    dim_scores: dict,
    61→) -> tuple[list[tuple[str, dict]], list[tuple[str, dict]]]:
    62→    # Build dimension table from canonical scorecard projection.
    63→    rows = scorecard_projection_mod.scorecard_dimension_rows(
    64→        state, dim_scores=dim_scores
    65→    )
    66→    subjective_name_set = {name.lower() for name in scoring_mod.DISPLAY_NAMES.values()}
    67→    subjective_name_set.update({"elegance", "elegance (combined)"})
    68→
    69→    mechanical = [
    70→        (name, data)
    71→        for name, data in rows
    72→        if (
    73→            "subjective_assessment" not in data.get("detectors", {})
    74→            and str(name).strip().lower() not in subjective_name_set
    75→        )
    76→    ]
    77→    subjective = [
    78→        (name, data)
    79→        for name, data in rows
    80→        if (
    81→            "subjective_assessment" in data.get("detectors", {})
    82→            or str(name).strip().lower() in subjective_name_set
    83→        )
    84→    ]
    85→    return mechanical, subjective
    86→
    87→
    88→def _print_dimension_table(state: dict, dim_scores: dict) -> None:
    89→    mechanical, subjective = _split_dimension_scores(state, dim_scores)
    90→    if not (mechanical or subjective):
    91→        return
    92→
    93→    print("| Dimension | Health | Strict | Issues | Tier | Action |")
    94→    print("|-----------|--------|--------|--------|------|--------|")
    95→    for name, data in sorted(mechanical, key=lambda item: item[0]):
    96→        score = data.get("score", 100)
    97→        strict = data.get("strict", score)
    98→        issues = data.get("issues", 0)
    99→        tier = data.get("tier", "")
   100→        action = registry_mod.dimension_action_type(name)
   101→        print(
   102→            f"| {name} | {score:.1f}% | {strict:.1f}% | {issues} | T{tier} | {action} |"
   103→        )
   104→    if subjective:
   105→        print("| **Subjective Dimensions** | | | | | |")
   106→        for name, data in sorted(subjective, key=lambda item: item[0]):
   107→            score = data.get("score", 100)
   108→            strict = data.get("strict", score)
   109→            issues = data.get("issues", 0)
   110→            tier = data.get("tier", "")
   111→            print(
   112→                f"| {name} | {score:.1f}% | {strict:.1f}% | {issues} | T{tier} | review |"
   113→            )
   114→    print()
   115→
   116→
   117→def _print_stats_summary(
   118→    state: dict,
   119→    diff: dict | None,
   120→    *,
   121→    overall_score: float | None,
   122→    strict_score: float | None,
   123→) -> None:
   124→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
   125→    if not stats:
   126→        return
   127→
   128→    wontfix = stats.get("wontfix", 0)
   129→    ignored = diff.get("ignored", 0) if diff else 0
   130→    ignore_pats = diff.get("ignore_patterns", 0) if diff else 0
   131→    strict_gap = (
   132→        round((overall_score or 0) - (strict_score or 0), 1)
   133→        if overall_score and strict_score
   134→        else 0
   135→    )
   136→    print(
   137→        f"Total findings: {stats.get('total', 0)} | "
   138→        f"Open: {stats.get('open', 0)} | "
   139→        f"Fixed: {stats.get('fixed', 0)} | "
   140→        f"Wontfix: {wontfix}"
   141→    )
   142→    if wontfix or ignored or ignore_pats:
   143→        print(
   144→            f"Ignored: {ignored} (by {ignore_pats} patterns) | Strict gap: {strict_gap} pts"
   145→        )
   146→        print("Focus on strict score — wontfix and ignore inflate the lenient score.")
   147→    print()
   148→
   149→
   150→_WORKFLOW_GUIDE = build_workflow_guide(ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   151→
   152→
   153→def _print_workflow_guide() -> None:
   154→    # Workflow guide — teach agents the full cycle
   155→    print(_WORKFLOW_GUIDE)
   156→    print()
   157→
   158→
   159→def _print_narrative_status(narrative: dict | None) -> None:
   160→    if not narrative:
   161→        return
   162→
   163→    headline = narrative.get("headline", "")
   164→    strategy = narrative.get("strategy") or {}
   165→    actions = narrative.get("actions", [])
   166→    if headline:
   167→        print(f"Current status: {headline}")
   168→    hint = strategy.get("hint", "")
   169→    if hint:
   170→        print(f"Strategy: {hint}")
   171→    if actions:
   172→        top = actions[0]
   173→        print(f"Top action: `{top['command']}` — {top['description']}")
   174→    print()
   175→
   176→
   177→def _detect_agent_interface() -> str | None:
   178→    """Detect the current agent interface from environment variables."""
   179→    if os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE"):
   180→        return "claude"
   181→    if os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI"):
   182→        return "gemini"
   183→    if os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED") or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX"):
   184→        return "codex"
   185→    if os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID"):
   186→        return "cursor"
   187→    return None
   188→
   189→
   190→def _try_auto_update_skill() -> None:
   191→    """Attempt to auto-install or auto-update the skill document.
   192→
   193→    Best-effort: swallows all exceptions so a network failure or permission
   194→    error never breaks the scan.
   195→    """
   196→    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
   197→
   198→    if install and not install.stale:
   199→        return  # Up to date.
   200→
   201→    try:
   202→        from desloppify.app.commands.update_skill import (
   203→            resolve_interface,
   204→            update_installed_skill,
   205→        )
   206→
   207→        if install:
   208→            interface = resolve_interface(install=install)
   209→        else:
   210→            interface = _detect_agent_interface()
   211→
   212→        if interface:
   213→            update_installed_skill(interface)
   214→    except (ImportError, OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError) as exc:
   215→        logger.debug("Skill auto-update skipped: %s", exc, exc_info=True)
   216→
   217→
   218→def _print_badge_hint(badge_path: Path | None) -> None:
   219→    if not (badge_path and badge_path.exists()):
   220→        return
   221→
   222→    rel_path = badge_path.name if badge_path.parent == PROJECT_ROOT else str(badge_path)
   223→    print(f"A scorecard image was saved to `{rel_path}`.")
   224→    print("Let the user know they can view it, and suggest adding it")
   225→    print(f'to their README: `<img src="{rel_path}" width="100%">`')
   226→
   227→
   228→def _print_llm_summary(
   229→    state: dict,
   230→    badge_path: Path | None,
   231→    narrative: dict | None = None,
   232→    diff: dict | None = None,
   233→):
   234→    """Print a structured summary for LLM consumption.
   235→
   236→    The LLM reads terminal output after running scans. This gives it
   237→    clear instructions on how to present the results to the end user.
   238→    Only shown when running inside an agent (CLAUDE_CODE or DESLOPPIFY_AGENT env).
   239→    """
   240→    if not _is_agent_environment():
   241→        return
   242→
   243→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   244→    scores = _load_scores(state)
   245→
   246→    if (
   247→        scores.overall is None
   248→        and scores.objective is None
   249→        and scores.strict is None
   250→        and scores.verified is None
   251→        and not dim_scores
   252→    ):
   253→        return
   254→
   255→    print("─" * 60)
   256→    print("INSTRUCTIONS FOR LLM")
   257→    print("IMPORTANT: ALWAYS present ALL scores to the user after a scan.")
   258→    print("Show overall health (lenient + strict), ALL dimension scores,")
   259→    print("AND all subjective dimension scores in a markdown table.")
   260→    print("The goal is to maximize strict scores. Never skip the scores.\n")
   261→
   262→    _print_score_lines(
   263→        overall_score=scores.overall,
   264→        objective_score=scores.objective,
   265→        strict_score=scores.strict,
   266→        verified_score=scores.verified,
   267→    )
   268→    _print_dimension_table(state, dim_scores)
   269→    _print_stats_summary(
   270→        state,
   271→        diff,
   272→        overall_score=scores.overall,
   273→        strict_score=scores.strict,
   274→    )
   275→    _print_workflow_guide()
   276→    _print_narrative_status(narrative)
   277→    _print_badge_hint(badge_path)
   278→    print("─" * 60)
   279→
   280→
   281→def auto_update_skill() -> None:
   282→    """Auto-install or update the skill document if we detect an agent.
   283→
   284→    Called unconditionally from the scan workflow — not gated on scores.
   285→    """
   286→    if not _is_agent_environment():
   287→        return
   288→
   289→    _try_auto_update_skill()
   290→
   291→    # Single post-check: whatever happened above, is the doc current now?
   292→    install = skill_docs_mod.find_installed_skill()
   293→    if not install:
   294→        names = ", ".join(sorted(skill_docs_mod.SKILL_TARGETS))
   295→        print(
   296→            f"No skill document found. Install one for better workflow guidance: "
   297→            f"desloppify update-skill <{names}>"
   298→        )
   299→    elif install.stale:
   300→        print(
   301→            f"Skill document is outdated "
   302→            f"(v{install.version}, current v{skill_docs_mod.SKILL_VERSION}). "
   303→            f"Run: desloppify update-skill"
   304→        )
   305→
   306→
   307→__all__ = ["_print_llm_summary", "auto_update_skill"]
   308→
```

```
    67→def _plan_dimension_table(state: PlanState) -> list[str]:

   171→
   172→def _plan_tier_sections(findings: dict, *, state: PlanState | None = None) -> list[str]:
   173→    """Build per-tier sections from the shared work-queue backend."""
   174→
   175→    queue_state: PlanState | dict = state or {"findings": findings}
   176→    scan_path = state.get("scan_path") if state else None
   177→    raw_target = (
   178→        (state or {}).get("config", {}).get("target_strict_score", 95)
   179→        if isinstance(state, dict)
   180→        else 95
   181→    )
   182→    try:
   183→        subjective_threshold = float(raw_target)
   184→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   185→        subjective_threshold = 95.0
   186→    subjective_threshold = max(0.0, min(100.0, subjective_threshold))
   187→    if "findings" not in queue_state:
   188→        queue_state = {**queue_state, "findings": findings}
   189→
   190→    queue = build_work_queue(
   191→        queue_state,
   192→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   193→            count=None,
   194→            scan_path=scan_path,
   195→            status="open",
   196→            include_subjective=True,
   197→            subjective_threshold=subjective_threshold,
   198→            no_tier_fallback=True,
   199→        ),
   200→    )
   201→    open_items = queue.get("items", [])
   202→    by_tier_file: dict[int, dict[str, list]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
   203→    for item in open_items:
   204→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   205→        by_tier_file[tier][item.get("file", ".")].append(item)
   206→
   207→    lines: list[str] = []
   208→    for tier_num in [1, 2, 3, 4]:
   209→        tier_files = by_tier_file.get(tier_num, {})
   210→        if not tier_files:
   211→            continue
   212→
   213→        label = TIER_LABELS.get(tier_num, f"Tier {tier_num}")
   214→        tier_count = sum(len(file_findings) for file_findings in tier_files.values())
   215→        lines.extend(
   216→            [
   217→                "---",
   218→                f"## Tier {tier_num}: {label} ({tier_count} open)",
   219→                "",
   220→            ]
   221→        )
   222→
   223→        sorted_files = sorted(
   224→            tier_files.items(), key=lambda item: (-len(item[1]), item[0])
   225→        )
   226→        for filepath, file_items in sorted_files:
   227→            display_path = "Codebase-wide" if filepath == "." else filepath
   228→            lines.append(f"### `{display_path}` ({len(file_items)} findings)")
   229→            lines.append("")
   230→            for item in file_items:
   231→                if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   232→                    lines.append(f"- [ ] [subjective] {item.get('summary', '')}")
   233→                    lines.append(f"      `{item.get('id', '')}`")
   234→                    if item.get("primary_command"):
   235→                        lines.append(f"      action: `{item['primary_command']}`")
   236→                    continue
   237→
   238→                conf_badge = f"[{item.get('confidence', 'medium')}]"
   239→                lines.append(f"- [ ] {conf_badge} {item.get('summary', '')}")
   240→                lines.append(f"      `{item.get('id', '')}`")
   241→            lines.append("")
   242→
   243→    return lines
   244→
   245→
   246→def _tier_summary_lines(stats: dict) -> list[str]:
   247→    lines: list[str] = []
   248→    by_tier = stats.get("by_tier", {})
   249→    for tier_num in [1, 2, 3, 4]:
   250→        tier_stats = by_tier.get(str(tier_num), {})
   251→        open_count = tier_stats.get("open", 0)
   252→        total = sum(tier_stats.values())
   253→        addressed = total - open_count
   254→        pct = round(addressed / total * 100) if total else 100
   255→        label = TIER_LABELS.get(tier_num, f"Tier {tier_num}")
   256→        lines.append(
   257→            f"- **Tier {tier_num}** ({label}): {open_count} open / {total} total ({pct}% addressed)"
   258→        )
   259→    lines.append("")
   260→    return lines
   261→
   262→
   263→def _addressed_section(findings: dict) -> list[str]:
   264→    addressed = [
   265→        finding for finding in findings.values() if finding["status"] != "open"
   266→    ]
   267→    if not addressed:
   268→        return []
   269→
   270→    lines: list[str] = ["---", "## Addressed", ""]
   271→    by_status: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   272→    for finding in addressed:
   273→        by_status[finding["status"]] += 1
   274→    for status, count in sorted(by_status.items()):
   275→        lines.append(f"- **{status}**: {count}")
   276→
   277→    wontfix = [
   278→        finding
   279→        for finding in addressed
   280→        if finding["status"] == "wontfix" and finding.get("note")
   281→    ]
   282→    if wontfix:
   283→        lines.extend(["", "### Wontfix (with explanations)", ""])
   284→        for finding in wontfix[:30]:
   285→            lines.append(f"- `{finding['id']}` — {finding['note']}")
   286→
   287→    lines.append("")
   288→    return lines
   289→
   290→
   291→def generate_plan_md(state: PlanState) -> str:
   292→    """Generate a prioritized markdown plan from state."""
   293→    findings = state["findings"]
   294→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
   295→
   296→    lines = _plan_header(state, stats)
   297→    lines.extend(_plan_dimension_table(state))
   298→    lines.extend(_tier_summary_lines(stats))
   299→    lines.extend(_plan_tier_sections(findings, state=state))
   300→    lines.extend(_addressed_section(findings))
   301→
   302→    return "\n".join(lines)
   303→
```

```
    15→from desloppify.core.tooling import check_tool_staleness
    16→
    17→from .payload import ShowPayloadMeta, build_show_payload
    18→from .render import (
    19→    render_findings,
    20→    show_agent_plan,
    21→    show_subjective_followup,
    22→    write_show_output_file,
    23→)
    24→from .scope import (
    25→    _detector_names_hint,
    26→    load_matches,
    27→    load_matches_for_dimension,
    28→    resolve_noise,
    29→    resolve_show_scope,
    30→)
    31→
    32→
    33→def _show_concerns(state: dict, lang_name: str | None) -> None:
    34→    """Render current design concerns from mechanical signals."""
    35→    from desloppify.engine.concerns import generate_concerns
    36→
    37→    concerns = generate_concerns(state, lang_name=lang_name)
    38→    if not concerns:
    39→        print(colorize("  No design concerns detected.", "green"))
    40→        return
    41→
    42→    print(colorize(f"\n  {len(concerns)} design concern(s):\n", "bold"))
    43→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
    44→
    45→    for i, c in enumerate(concerns, 1):
    46→        print(colorize(f"  {i}. [{c.type}] {c.file}", "cyan"))
    47→        print(f"     {c.summary}")
    48→        for ev in c.evidence:
    49→            print(colorize(f"       - {ev}", "dim"))
    50→        print(colorize(f"     ? {c.question}", "yellow"))
    51→
    52→        # Check if previously dismissed (but resurface due to changed findings).
    53→        prev = dismissals.get(c.fingerprint)
    54→        if isinstance(prev, dict):
    55→            reasoning = prev.get("reasoning", "")
    56→            if reasoning:
    57→                print(colorize(f"     (previously dismissed: {reasoning})", "dim"))
    58→        print()
    59→
    60→
    61→def cmd_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    62→    """Show all findings for a file, directory, detector, or pattern."""
    63→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    64→    state = runtime.state
    65→    config = runtime.config
    66→
    67→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    68→        return
    69→
    70→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    71→    if stale_warning:
    72→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
    73→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
    74→    if skill_warning:
    75→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
    76→
    77→    # Handle "show concerns" as a special view.
    78→    pattern_raw = getattr(args, "pattern", "")
    79→    if pattern_raw and pattern_raw.strip().lower() == "concerns":
    80→        lang = resolve_lang(args)
    81→        _show_concerns(state, lang.name if lang else None)
    82→        return
    83→
    84→    show_code = getattr(args, "code", False)
    85→    chronic = getattr(args, "chronic", False)
    86→    ok, pattern, status_filter, scope = resolve_show_scope(args)
    87→    if not ok or pattern is None:
    88→        return
    89→
    90→    matches = load_matches(state, scope=scope, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=chronic)
    91→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    92→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
    93→    narrative = compute_narrative(
    94→        state,
    95→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="show"),
    96→    )
    97→
    98→    if not matches:
    99→        # Try interpreting the pattern as a dimension name/key
   100→        dim_matches, dim_display = load_matches_for_dimension(
   101→            state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter
   102→        )
   103→        if dim_matches:
   104→            matches = dim_matches
   105→            pattern = dim_display or pattern
   106→        else:
   107→            # "show subjective" with no findings still shows the subjective dashboard
   108→            is_subjective_view = pattern_raw and pattern_raw.strip().lower() in (
   109→                "subjective", "subjective_review",
   110→            )
   111→            if is_subjective_view:
   112→                print(colorize(f"No {status_filter} findings matching: {pattern}", "yellow"))
   113→                write_query(
   114→                    {
   115→                        "command": "show",
   116→                        "query": pattern,
   117→                        "status_filter": status_filter,
   118→                        "total": 0,
   119→                        "findings": [],
   120→                        "narrative": narrative,
   121→                    }
   122→                )
   123→                show_subjective_followup(
   124→                    state,
   125→                    target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0),
   126→                )
   127→                return
   128→
   129→            hint = _detector_names_hint()
   130→            print(
   131→                colorize(
   132→                    f"No {status_filter} findings matching: {pattern}",
   133→                    "yellow",
   134→                )
   135→            )
   136→            print(
   137→                colorize(
   138→                    f"  Try: show <detector>, show <file>, or show subjective. "
   139→                    f"Detectors: {hint}",
   140→                    "dim",
   141→                )
   142→            )
   143→            write_query(
   144→                {
   145→                    "command": "show",
   146→                    "query": pattern,
   147→                    "status_filter": status_filter,
   148→                    "total": 0,
   149→                    "findings": [],
   150→                    "narrative": narrative,
   151→                }
   152→            )
   153→            return
   154→
   155→    (
   156→        surfaced_matches,
   157→        hidden_by_detector,
   158→        noise_budget,
   159→        global_noise_budget,
   160→        budget_warning,
   161→    ) = resolve_noise(
   162→        config,
   163→        matches,
   164→    )
   165→    hidden_total = sum(hidden_by_detector.values())
   166→
   167→    payload = build_show_payload(
   168→        surfaced_matches,
   169→        pattern,
   170→        status_filter,
   171→        ShowPayloadMeta(
   172→            total_matches=len(matches),
   173→            hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   174→            noise_budget=noise_budget,
   175→            global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   176→        ),
   177→    )
   178→    write_query({"command": "show", **payload, "narrative": narrative})
   179→
   180→    output_file = getattr(args, "output", None)
   181→    if output_file:
   182→        if write_show_output_file(output_file, payload, len(surfaced_matches)):
   183→            return
   184→        raise SystemExit(1)
   185→
   186→    top = getattr(args, "top", 20) or 20
   187→    render_findings(
   188→        surfaced_matches,
   189→        pattern=pattern,
   190→        status_filter=status_filter,
   191→        show_code=show_code,
   192→        top=top,
   193→        hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   194→        hidden_total=hidden_total,
   195→        noise_budget=noise_budget,
   196→        global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   197→        budget_warning=budget_warning,
   198→    )
   199→    show_agent_plan(narrative, surfaced_matches)
   200→    show_subjective_followup(
   201→        state,
   202→        target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0),
   203→    )
   204→
   205→    # Phase 5: naming guide for subjective views
   206→    if pattern_raw and pattern_raw.strip().lower() in ("subjective", "subjective_review"):
   207→        print(colorize("  Related views:", "dim"))
   208→        print(colorize("    `show review --status open`            Per-file design review findings", "dim"))
   209→        print(colorize("    `show subjective_review --status open`  Files needing re-review", "dim"))
   210→
   211→
   212→__all__ = ["cmd_show"]
   213→
```
